Header injection is not a concern because the payload goes to Resend as JSON rather than raw SMTP. Verified that `esc(undefined)` returns an empty string, so the existing `esc(page)` or-else `'N/A'` fallback still behaves exactly as before |
| C3L-85, **open, Low, observed 2026-08-06, recorded rather than tested to conclusion** | **Nothing limits how many accounts one person can create, so the 100-follow cap is per account rather than per person.** `FREE_FOLLOW_CAP = 100` is correctly centralised in `accounts-core.mjs` and enforced in the one write path, which is the right design and closes the drift C3L-49's era of inline rules created. What it does not do, and does not claim to, is bind a cap to a human being. Multi-account gaming is therefore possible in principle | Read from `accounts-core.mjs` (`capFor(tier)`, the single insert path) plus the observation that account creation requires only an address that can receive a confirmation link. **No attempt was made to actually create multiple accounts and exercise the bypass**, because doing so means sending real confirmation emails and writing synthetic rows to production, which this batch's read-only posture excludes | **Low, and honestly of little consequence today.** A follow costs C3 an email per triggered alert, so the abuse ceiling is bounded by how many addresses an attacker controls, and there is no paid tier or scarce resource to game for advantage. **Recorded so it is on the record before a paid tier exists**, at which point per-person rather than per-account limits stop being academic. Stated plainly: this is an observation from reading, not a demonstrated exploit |
| C3L-86, **open, Medium rising to High on a date, confirmed and projected 2026-08-06, task `c3-audit-8-perf`** | **The price snapshot tables grow without bound and nothing anywhere prunes them.** `mtg_price_snapshots` holds **4,488,971 rows across 86 distinct days** (2026-05-04 to today) at **1,594 MB**, and gains a full new day every day. There is **no retention policy, no pruning job and no archival step** in the 11 pg_cron jobs, the 31 scheduled Netlify functions, the migrations directory or `scripts/`. This is not a defect today; it is a dated one | **Measured, then projected, which is what protocol Section 16.2 asks for instead of load testing.** Per-day growth computed from real row counts times real bytes-per-row: **mtg 18.70 MB/day**, pokemon 3.08, yugioh 2.93, vanguard 1.73, digimon 0.89, dragonball 0.82. **Top six alone: 28.15 MB/day.** Database currently **3,832 MB**. Against Supabase Pro's 8 GB included disk that is **4,360 MB of headroom, roughly 140 days, so approximately late December 2026** at the measured rate | **Not fixed, and nothing here could be: every remedy is a schema or data change that deletes production history.** The decision to make is what retention actually serves the product. Concrete input for it: the card page charts a **90 day** window (`price-chart.mjs`), and the price-change functions look back **30 days** at most, **so nothing on the site reads a snapshot older than 90 days**, while 86 days are held and growing indefinitely. A 180 day retention would preserve every current consumer with double margin and cap the largest table at roughly its present size. **Honest caveats: the 8 GB ceiling is the standard Pro allowance and was NOT verified against this project's actual plan**, so the date moves if the plan differs; and the projection assumes today's card counts, which grow as sets release |
| C3L-87, **open, Medium, measured 2026-08-06** | **The working set no longer fits in RAM, and MTG is where it shows.** `shared_buffers` is **512 MB**, while the two heavily-used indexes on `mtg_price_snapshots` alone total **736 MB** (`..._scryfall_id_snapshot_date_key` 400 MB with 1.64M scans, `idx_mtg_snapshots_date_scryfall` 336 MB with 1.97M scans). The consequence is visible in the buffer statistics: **`mtg_price_snapshots` has a heap cache hit ratio of 88.24 per cent with 48.1 million block reads served from disk**, and `mtg_cards` sits at 90.91 per cent. A healthy figure for this shape of workload is above 99 per cent | `pg_settings` for `shared_buffers` (65536 8kB pages), `pg_stat_user_indexes` for per-index size and scan counts, `pg_statio_user_tables` for hit ratios. **This also gives C3L-78 a mechanism**: six yugioh set pages 503ing at concurrency 6 while passing individually is what disk-bound reads under concurrent cold requests look like, rather than pure function cold start | **Not fixed, not fixable from the repo.** The two large indexes are **NOT redundant despite covering the same two columns**, because they are in opposite order and a `(a,b)` index cannot serve a query filtering on `b` alone; both show millions of scans and **both should be kept**, which is stated explicitly so a future pass does not "optimise" one away. The real levers are a larger instance or a smaller table, and **C3L-86's retention policy is the cheaper of the two**, since cutting the snapshot table would shrink these indexes proportionally. **Honest caveat: `pg_statio` counters are cumulative since the last stats reset, so 88.24 per cent is a long-run average and NOT a measurement of current load.** One tidy-up noted in passing: `mtg_price_snapshots_pkey` is **172 MB and has been scanned 8 times**, because real identity is the `(scryfall_id, snapshot_date)` unique constraint; dropping a primary key is a schema change and is mentioned as context for the retention task, not proposed |
| C3L-88, **open, Medium, measured 2026-08-06, FIGURE REVISED 2026-08-07, and the diagnostic behind it is now compromised going forward** | **Roughly two thirds of C3's own recorded card views carry a machine signature, and nothing distinguishes them from human traffic.** Grouping `card_views` by game and dividing views by distinct sessions gives **mtg 6.5 views per session across 898 sessions**, which is what browsing looks like, and **exactly 1.0 for every other game of any volume**: vanguard 5,467 views across 5,467 distinct sessions, forceofwill 2,762 / 2,762, wow 1,316 / 1,316, gundam 493 / 493, digimon 2,508 / 2,507. **Zero repeat views in 5,467 vanguard visits is not human behaviour**; it is a client that starts with empty storage every request. The naive read of this table, that Vanguard is C3's second most popular game ahead of Pokemon and Yu-Gi-Oh, **is an artefact** | The 1:1 signature is the evidence, and the alternative explanation was checked and ruled out: **the session mechanism is IDENTICAL for all 32 games.** `shared/view-tracking.mjs` and MTG's own `card-page.mjs` both read and write the same `localStorage` key `c3_session` with the same generator, so a code difference cannot explain why only MTG shows session reuse. Views are only recorded when JavaScript runs, so this is JS-executing crawler traffic with a fresh context per page, not simple HTTP scraping | **Not fixed, and no code change would fix it, which is why it is logged as a reading correction rather than a defect.** What it needs is a bot filter or a signal to separate the two populations, and the 1:1 ratio itself is a usable one. **Consequences worth carrying: any "top games" ranking taken from this table is wrong**, four Extended games account for about 63 per cent of recorded views while Core 8 accounts for roughly 32 per cent, and that comparison is between real MTG traffic and machine traffic elsewhere. It also means C3L-86's disk growth is partly serving crawlers. **Honest caveat: "bot" is an inference from the session signature, strong but indirect.** No user agent is stored in `card_views`, so it cannot be confirmed from this table alone, and capturing one would be the cheapest way to settle it. **FIGURE REVISED 2026-08-07: the bot share is 86.3 per cent, not "roughly two thirds".** Only **13.7 per cent of all-time `card_views` predates the 22 July crawler onset**, so the earlier fraction understated it. **More important than the number: this diagnostic is now compromised for current data.** The crawler reached MTG on 6 August, which is the game the 1:1 views-per-session method leaned on as a clean comparison, so **the method no longer separates real traffic from bot traffic on anything recent. It remains valid only on data cut before 22 July.** Any future use of this ratio must state which side of that date it is measuring, or it will simply re-measure the crawler and call it a signature |
| C3L-89, **RESOLVED 2026-08-07, verified in production, not inferred from the deploy** | **`card_views.country_code` is not a measurement. It is the string `'AU'` written by a literal on every insert.** `card-api.mjs:176` sets `country_code: 'AU'` unconditionally, so **all 18,990 rows say Australia** regardless of where the request came from, including the crawler traffic in C3L-88 | `SELECT country_code, count(*) ... GROUP BY 1` returns exactly one row, `AU` with 18,990. Note for that task: real geo is available but **not from this handler's signature**, which is `export default async (req)` with no `context` argument, so Netlify's geo would need threading through the dispatcher rather than a one-line swap. **CLOSED 2026-08-07.** The fingerprint module shipped in `f6b0e5a` is confirmed live in production and writing real values: **16,795 rows checked, 0 carrying the old fabricated `'AU'` literal**. The column now holds measured geo rather than a hardcoded string. **Verified by querying the live table rather than by observing that the deploy published**, which is the distinction this register keeps insisting on and which is why this is closed rather than assumed closed |
| C3L-90, **RESOLVED 2026-08-06, task `c3-audit-signup-abuse`, three layers plus a kill switch, all tested.**. **Original entry folded into this row, per the Section 0 rule that status is updated in place rather than a second row appended. Original status:** ~~**open, HIGH, measured 2026-08-06, task `c3-audit-10-alerts`. This one is happening right now**~~ | **Defences shipped in the order the task set, each verified before the next.** **Step A**, a temporary `SIGNUP_CONFIRM_EMAIL_ENABLED` kill switch, set false to stop the bleeding while the real fixes were built, **and set back to TRUE in Step E**, verified by test. It gates **both** branches of `handleSignup`, not just the new-account one, and that is the significant decision: **gating only the confirm send would have reopened C3L-44**, because the registered branch would still make an outbound Resend call while the unregistered one did not, which is the same timing separation C3L-44 measured at 491ms and closed. **Step B**, honeypot: an off-screen `website` input, `aria-hidden`, `tabindex="-1"`, `autocomplete="off"`. Positioned off-screen rather than `display:none` deliberately, since that is the first thing a scraper checks. A populated field returns **the identical `checkEmailPage` a real success returns**, so there is no tell to adapt around. **Step C**, per-IP limit, 5 per hour, keyed on `x-nf-client-connection-ip` with an `x-forwarded-for` first-entry fallback. **This is the axis that actually matters here**: `maySendLink` is keyed on the ADDRESS, and 180 signups on 180 different addresses reset it every time and never once fired it. **Step D** closes C3L-92 at the same time, see its row **Superseded, original text kept:** ~~**The signup form is being farmed by bots, and every signup makes C3 send a confirmation email from its own verified domain to an address that never asked for it.** 181 accounts exist. **Exactly ONE of them has ever created a follow.** The other 180 signed up for a card-following product and followed nothing. The timeline is not gradual: 1 account on 16 July, then **180 in the eight days from 30 July** (26, 19, 12, 12, 29, 38, 36, 8), and the domain mix on the heavy days is dominated by obscure and corporate addresses rather than consumer ones (24 of 38 on 4 August, 17 of 29 on 3 August), including clusters of 2 to 4 accounts on one company domain on a single day and throwaway domains such as `immenseignite.info`. **Zero follow conversion across 180 accounts is not a funnel problem, it is not humans**~~ | **Tested by running the real handler, not by reading it.** `scripts/test-signup-defences.mjs` stubs `Netlify.env` and `fetch` so the module loads and every outbound call is intercepted, then asserts behaviour: **25 assertions, all passing.** A real signup still returns 200 with the check-your-email page and no error; a filled honeypot creates **0 accounts and sends 0 emails** while returning a response indistinguishable from success; an empty honeypot still creates the account; the IP limit **first blocks on attempt 6** and a different IP is unaffected. **The suite was then proved to be a real guard rather than a rubber stamp**: disabling the honeypot fails it 2 ways, disabling the IP limit fails it 2 ways, removing the `email_log` write fails it 1 way, and restoring all three returns 25 of 25 **Superseded, original text kept:** ~~Account rows grouped by day and by email domain, cross-referenced against `follows` (4 rows, all belonging to a single `user_id`) and `follow_magic_links` (183 rows, 155 expired), which is consistent with one confirmation link generated and emailed per signup. **The send is confirmed by reading the code path**: `account.mjs` calls `if (maySendLink(email)) await sendLinkEmail(...)` on signup, and `maySendLink` is keyed on the submitted address, so a fresh address passes every time~~ | **Resolved and wired into CI** (`syntax-check.yml`), so the defences cannot regress silently. **Honest limits, stated rather than implied. (1) All three limiters in this file, including the new one, are in-memory and per serverless instance**, so they blunt a burst from one origin and do NOT stop an attempt distributed across many IPs; a shared counter needs a database or KV store and is the upgrade if this proves insufficient. **(2) The honeypot stops naive bots only**, which is exactly what it is for; anything that renders CSS will skip it. **(3) Turnstile was requested and is NOT built**, because no Turnstile key exists, there is no `wrangler` and no `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, and registering a Cloudflare account is not something to do unattended on someone's behalf. **The seam is clean: one more check in `handleSignup` alongside the honeypot.** (4) The 180 accounts already created were **not deleted**, that is its own decision. **(5) UPDATE 6 August: the delivery question this row left open is now CLOSED and measured, see C3L-97. 185 emails, 7 bounced, 1 complaint, a 0.54 per cent complaint rate against the 0.3 per cent industry threshold. The harm was real and is now quantified rather than inferred** **Superseded, original text kept:** ~~**HIGH, not fixed, not contained.** The sharp point is about the defence that already exists: **C3L-46's throttle is per address, which is the wrong axis for this attack.** A bot using 180 different addresses never trips it once, so the protection added by two earlier findings does not apply here at all. Real remedies (a captcha, a honeypot field, per-IP limiting, or requiring confirmation before the account row persists) are all feature work needing a decision about signup friction. **The cost is the same one C3L-83 warns about but already realised: roughly 180 unsolicited emails from C3's sending domain in eight days**, which is exactly how a domain's reputation degrades and takes the weekly digest and the follow alerts down with it~~ |

FROM sync_events WHERE game or event_type matches follow, alert or digest` returns an empty set. `follows` shows 4 rows, 4 confirmed, **0 triggered** | **Not fixed, and deliberately so on precedent.** Adding a new event stream is easy, but Task 19 already answered this shape when it declined to widen the sync health check with an unrequested signal, on the grounds that it would muddy what the check's next red run means. | C3L-93, **RESOLVED 2026-08-06, task `c3-audit-ev-takedown`, Task 24 Piece 4. Three changes were needed, not one.**. **Original entry folded into this row, per the Section 0 rule that status is updated in place rather than a second row appended. Original status:** ~~**open, HIGH, confirmed 2026-08-06, task `c3-audit-11-integration`. A documented integration that has never produced a single row in production**~~ | **A schedule alone would not have fixed this, and that is the finding underneath the finding.** (1) The **auth guard** required `x-sync-secret` unconditionally, and Netlify's scheduler sends no custom headers, so every scheduled run would have answered **401**. Adding a schedule without this would have swapped one silent no-op for another. It now accepts a request with no `x-sync-secret`, no `origin` and no `referer` as scheduled, the shape copied from the 31 syncs that provably fire. (2) It wrote **no telemetry at all**, which is how it went weeks without running unnoticed; it now writes `sync_start` and exactly one terminal event under game `apitcg-enrichment`, so C3L-51's health check can see it, with **quota exhaustion logged as `sync_error` rather than success**. (3) The schedule itself is **`0 13 * * 0`**, Sunday 13:00 UTC **Superseded, original text kept:** ~~**The apitcg.com enrichment has enriched ZERO cards across all five of its games.** `digimon` 0 of 9,316, `unionarena` 0 of 6,453, `dbsfusionworld` 0 of 4,109, `gundam` 0 of 1,683, `riftbound` 0 of 1,295. **22,856 cards, 0 enriched.** The cause is visible in one line: `enrich-apitcg-stats-background.mjs` declares **`export const config = {};`**, an empty object, so it has **no `schedule` and no `path`**. It cannot be fired by the scheduler and it is not routable, which means there is no way for it to run in production at all. **It is user-visible in exactly the C3L-61 shape**: `digimon-card-page.mjs` builds a "Card Details" grid from `custom_attributes` behind a truthiness guard, so the whole section is silently absent on all 22,856 of those card pages rather than showing an error~~ | Quota math done rather than quoted: a full five-game pass is **roughly 219 requests** against a free-plan **1,000 per month**. Daily would need about **6,570**. Weekly costs about **876** in a four-Sunday month. **The edge is stated rather than left to be found: a five-Sunday month reaches about 1,095 and exceeds the allowance by roughly 95.** 13:00 was chosen by checking every scheduled function in the repo and finding it the only hour carrying none **Superseded, original text kept:** ~~**The contrast is what makes this precise. The four FREE community APIs added in task-48 all work**, measured in the same pass: YGOPRODeck 19,555 of 47,071 yugioh cards (41.5 per cent), optcgapi 5,582 of 7,019 onepiece (79.5), swu-db 3,988 of 7,879 starwars (50.6), Lorcast 575 of 3,403 lorcana (16.9). **The single keyed, quota-limited source is the only one at zero.** The wipe mechanism DATA_SOURCES.md warns about was checked and is NOT the cause: all five `sync-{game}-background.mjs` files carry only a comment where the `custom_attributes` write used to be, so task-57's fix held~~ | **Resolved in code, NOT yet proven by a run**, and that distinction matters given this finding is precisely about a function that looked fine and had never executed. **First real test is the next Sunday 13:00 UTC slot.** Expected: a `sync_start` then `sync_success` under `apitcg-enrichment` in `sync_events`, and non-empty `custom_attributes` appearing across the five games. **A start with no terminal is the C3L-57 shape**; a `sync_error` naming quota exhaustion means the allowance was already spent that month. The five-Sunday overrun is accepted rather than designed around: it lands days before the monthly reset, and a day-of-month form like `1,8,15,22` would guarantee four runs but **C3L-64 is an open finding about exactly this repo silently not firing an unusual cron expression** **Superseded, original text kept:** ~~**HIGH, not fixed, not contained.** Making it run means choosing how: a schedule, a `path` with the standard `x-sync-secret` guard, or a one-off backfill. **That choice is constrained by a real limit rather than free**: the plan is 1,000 requests per month and a full five-game run costs about 219, so a daily schedule would need roughly 6,570 and blow the quota six times over. A monthly or weekly cadence is the only affordable shape, which is a decision. **The register documents this integration as working, quoting match rates of 100, 99 and 95 per cent**, and those figures were evidently measured during development rather than from production data, so DATA_SOURCES.md needs correcting in the same task~~  **RE-OPENED AND RE-DIAGNOSED 2026-08-10 in Task 42, and the cause is NOT what the task file said.** The task file described an empty `export const config = {}` with neither schedule nor route. **That is stale: it carries `schedule: '0 13 * * 0'` and it does run.** It ran on **9 August at 13:00:54** and wrote its own diagnosis to `sync_events`: **`sync_error`, "apitcg monthly quota exhausted after 1 requests; games completed: 0/5"**. Confirmed independently by calling apitcg.com directly on 10 August: **every request returns HTTP 429 with `{"success":false,"error":"Request limit reached for this month."}`**, while the response headers show the per-minute limit barely touched (`limit=300, remaining=294`). So the key is valid, the code is correct, the instrumentation is correct, and **the blocker is budget, not engineering**. Enrichment remains **0 of 22,873 rows** across digimon, gundam, unionarena, riftbound and dbsfusionworld. **Data quality could NOT be assessed and NOTHING was wired in**, which is the honest outcome and matches the task own instruction to hold rather than wire in broken enrichment. **One change shipped: weekly to monthly.** The job is a full re-backfill with no incremental mode, and its own header puts one pass at about 219 requests against a stated 1,000/month free quota, so weekly consumed roughly the entire allowance to gain nothing. Monthly leaves about 780 requests of headroom for a pass to actually complete when the quota resets on 1 September. **It also cannot be run on demand: it is scheduled, so C3L-136 applies, and it takes a `?dryRun=1` option that the new admin trigger has no way to pass.** |

Last deploy to publish: `41af4dd` at **11:34 UTC**. Previews canceled at **12:04 UTC**. The very next production push, `2f1ce04` at **12:08 UTC**, is the one the health check found sitting in **`state=new`**, and nothing has published since. **The mechanism that fits every observation: canceled builds did not release their concurrency slots, so the account stayed pinned at 3 of 3 and every later build was admitted to the queue but never scheduled.** That explains the specific state, `new` rather than `error` or `building`, which is a build that was accepted and never started, and it explains why nothing self-recovered over more than an hour. **It also predicts that pressing Trigger Deploy does nothing**, because a fresh build queues behind the same exhausted slots, and that prediction holds: the published deploy id is **unchanged at `6a7470df`** after the attempt. **Stated as a well-supported hypothesis rather than a proven fact**, since Netlify's internal slot accounting is not visible from here; what is verified is the timing, the 3 of 3, the `state=new`, and the unchanged deploy id. **HIGH while it lasts, and NOT actioned from this side.** Retrying a stuck deploy is a production action on live infrastructure and it was left for Sammy rather than triggered unattended. **The consequence worth stating plainly: Task 24's own verification step, "fetch a sample after deploy and confirm noindex is present", CANNOT be completed, so the EV takedown is verified in the repo and NOT verified live**, and this register should not record it as live until it is. **The one good thing here is that C3L-26 worked exactly as designed.** Task 09 built it, Task 09's own blind-spot note said its mismatch branch had never fired against a real bad deploy and only its missing-secret path was proven, and it has now fired correctly on the real thing. **RESOLUTION: deploy `6a74863a` published at 13:05:28 UTC**, commit `865d6ad`, `state=ready`, `error_message` null, titled "47 new files uploaded", which is the 38 EV pages plus the rest. **All six stranded commits are inside it**, confirmed by ancestry rather than assumed. Total outage of the deploy pipeline: **11:34 to 13:05 UTC, about 91 minutes.** **It cleared without intervention from this side**, consistent with the slot hypothesis: a stuck build slot that eventually timed out and released. **A caveat kept rather than dropped now that it is resolved: `state=new` and the 3 of 3 are still the only evidence for the mechanism**, and nothing was observed releasing the slot, so "the slots timed out" is the reading that fits, not something witnessed. **ADDED 2026-08-06 by task `c3-audit-traffic-spike`, from the Netlify deploy list: the stall did NOT end in a clean drain, and "cleared without intervention" is true only of the pipeline, not of the queued work.** Four deploys ended in **`state=error`** before any succeeded: `6a748158` (`a204392`), `6a7481de` (`f6b0e5a`), `6a7483a1` (`a7691af`) and `6a74854d` (no `commit_ref`). The three that published, `6a74863a` (`865d6ad`, 13:05:28), `6a748693` (`fdc7b5c`, 13:06:48) and `6a748734` (`888d913`, 13:09:19), are all LATER commits. **So the stranded deploys were discarded rather than drained**, and the reason nothing was lost is only that a git deploy ships the whole tree, so a later build carried the earlier commits' content. **This strengthens the slot reading rather than weakening it**, since queued builds erroring on release is what slot starvation looks like, but it means the recovery should not be described as the queue simply catching up |
| C3L-102, **RESOLVED 2026-08-06, task `c3-audit-c3l61-backfill-resilience`, and it is a defect in something this programme shipped four hours earlier** | **The enrichment backfill's first real run burned five sets.** It wrote a progress row for every set it looked at, which permanently removed that set from the queue, with no distinction between "enriched" and "merely attempted". All three were recorded as finished having done nothing and would never have been retried, and nothing recorded that they had been skipped. Separately, a single `pokemontcg.io 500 for set base4` threw **12 seconds into the run** and aborted every remaining set | Read from `sync_events` (`sync_start @ 12:40:21`, `sync_error @ 12:40:33`) and from the five `pokemon_enrichment_progress` rows joined against `pokemon_sets`, which showed `cards_updated = 0` against real card counts. **Fix verified by test, not by reading**: `classifySetOutcome` was extracted as a pure function and `scripts/test-enrichment-outcome.mjs` asserts all five burned sets by their real values, the 500, the two genuine no-ops that must NOT retry forever, and the queue ordering. 11 cases, all passing, wired into `syntax-check.yml`. **The test was checked for teeth: under the pre-fix logic 4 of 4 failure cases come back marked done having enriched nothing** | Resolved, three changes. A set is done only if it enriched cards or genuinely has none; anything else records `needs_retry`. Each set is attempted in its own try, so an upstream failure costs one set rather than the run. The five burned rows were deleted so they requeue. **One judgement call beyond the brief, stated because it matters: simply not recording a failed set would park it at the front of the queue forever and starve everything behind it**, so failures are recorded but flagged, with `MAX_ATTEMPTS` of 3 dropping an exhausted set back into ordinary rotation. A run where every attempted set needs a retry now logs `sync_error`, so it reaches the C3L-51 health check instead of looking like quiet progress |

Until then, affected sets will attempt 3 times, flag `needs_retry`, and fall into ordinary rotation rather than being lost, which is the safe failure C3L-102 bought |
| C3L-104, **FIXED 2026-08-06 across 31 sitemap functions, same task. It explains the crawl volume; it is NOT the cost problem, and that distinction is the point of this row** | **Every sitemap on the site tells every crawler that the entire card catalogue changed today, and it will say the same thing again tomorrow, forever.** All 33 per-game sitemap functions build each URL as `lastmod = c.updated_at.slice(0,10)` with a hardcoded `<changefreq>daily</changefreq>`. The daily syncs write `updated_at` on **every row of every table on every run**, so `lastmod` advances to the current date across the whole catalogue each day, whether or not a single price or field actually changed. **C3 is not being crawled unusually hard. It is asking to be.** Every card URL C3 submits is advertised as freshly modified daily, the strongest re-crawl signal a site can emit, to an audience `robots.txt` widened on 12 and 16 July by unblocking 32 of 36 sitemaps and adding welcome groups for GPTBot, OAI-SearchBot, ChatGPT-User, ClaudeBot, Claude-Web and PerplexityBot. **CORRECTION, made while fixing this and recorded rather than quietly amended: an earlier version of this row said "the 363,259 card URLs", and that is wrong.** 363,259 is the total card count across the 32 game tables, but every card sitemap filters on `price_aud >= 5.00`, so the number actually submitted is **49,840**. The defect and its cause are unchanged; only its scale was overstated, by 7.3x | **Measured on the live site, not inferred from the code alone.** A fetch of `/api/sitemap-vanguard` returns 5,034 card URLs and the `lastmod` distribution across all 5,034 is a **single value, `2026-08-06`**, with no second date in the file. Confirmed in the database against the 5 and 6 August syncs: `updated_at >= 2026-08-05` covers **31,103 of 31,440** weissschwarz rows, **26,018 of 26,018** vanguard, **7,761 of 7,761** buddyfight, **6,589 of 6,589** shadowverse, **6,453 of 6,453** unionarena. weissschwarz's maximum `updated_at` is `2026-08-06 00:37:57`, **exactly the `sync_success` timestamp recorded for its first run after the 9 day freeze**, tying the sitemap churn to a dated, self-inflicted event. **Live crawler identity is now measured too, not inferred**, since C3L-99's capture began writing at 13:05:30: of the first 98 identified hits, **82 are `meta-externalagent/1.1` from Meta's own `57.141.18.0/24`**, rotating four OS strings but honestly self-declaring, and the remaining 16 are two Alibaba Cloud Singapore ranges sending plain Chrome with no bot token | **FIXED across 31 files, and the design decision was stated and approved before it shipped rather than chosen silently, because a change touching 31 files at once is exactly where a silent choice does damage.** **A reusable change signal already existed and no schema change was needed: `last_price_update`, present on all 31 non-MTG card tables.** Three properties were measured, not assumed. **It can only ever make `lastmod` OLDER**: across all 31 games, **0 have a newer `lastmod` after the change** and 15 are now more conservative. **It is genuinely coarser**: vanguard went from 1 distinct date across 5,034 URLs to 3, yugioh 8 to 31, pokemon 5 to 14. **It is complete for the submitted population**: 0 sitemap-eligible rows have it null, because a card only enters a sitemap if it has a price and therefore a price-write timestamp. **`<changefreq>daily</changefreq>` was DELETED rather than set to `weekly`**, deliberately: Google ignores `changefreq` outright, and substituting a different word would have swapped one unverified claim for another instead of removing a false one. `<priority>` was kept, being a relative hint rather than a factual assertion. **Measured result: 44,009 of 49,840 submitted URLs, 88.3 per cent, now carry a date spread.** **Stated limitation rather than glossed: 7 games still emit a single date** (starwars, godzilla, warhammer, dragonball, bakugan, buddyfight, unionarena, 5,831 URLs), because their price job writes one timestamp to the whole table, so `last_price_update` is a conservative floor and not a true per-card signal everywhere. Those 7 still improved in honesty, just not in spread. **Rejected: the `<game>_price_snapshots` tables**, which would give a real per-card signal but need an aggregate across the full catalogue on a runtime endpoint, against tables C3L-86 records as growing unbounded. **`sitemap-sets.mjs` was deliberately NOT bundled** and is flagged as its own item: same `lastmod = updated_at` pattern, but `changefreq: weekly` is at least plausible and it carries a few thousand URLs, not 49,840. **One consequence worth knowing before it surprises someone: if a sync breaks, `lastmod` now FREEZES rather than advancing.** That is correct, nothing is changing, but it makes a frozen sitemap a symptom of a stalled sync rather than a defect in itself, which matters given weissschwarz just came off a 9 day freeze. **The original framing of this row was also CORRECTED before it was first committed, which matters more than the finding.** An earlier draft treated this as an urgent cost problem and said the invocation figures were unobtainable from this machine. **Both claims were wrong and are retracted here rather than left to be discovered.** The figures ARE obtainable (see C3L-106) and they say the opposite: **team-wide web requests peaked at 201K on 29 July and have DECLINED through August, 83K, 77K, 62K, 51K, 83K, 60K for 1 to 6 August**, and web requests account for **218.4 credits of 6,379.8 consumed, 3.4 per cent**. **The real cost driver is production deploys at 3,360 credits, 53 per cent**, which is this audit programme's own push rate, not crawlers. **So the correct priority for this finding is hygiene, not urgency**: the `lastmod` fix is still right, because advertising a false daily change to a declared crawler that obeys `robots.txt` is simply inaccurate, and because `updated_at` meaning "a sync ran" rather than "something changed" breaks any honest what-changed query. **Do NOT ship an edge rate limit or a Cloudflare block on this evidence**: it would absorb load that is not costing anything, at real risk to Googlebot and to the deliberate task-126 AEO decision. **Honest caveat: this explains and amplifies the crawl, it does not prove the crawlers would be absent without it**, and the 98 identified hits are a five minute sample outside the 6 August spike window, so they are early evidence of composition, not proof of it |

The 12 card URLs tested are **real MTG slugs `meta-externalagent` actually fetched**, joined back from `card_views` to `mtg_cards`. **The fix adds 32 per-hub `Allow: /cards/<game>$` lines plus `Allow: /cards/*/sets/` and `Allow: /cards/weissschwarz/series/`.** One line per hub is not verbosity: **robots.txt cannot express "one path segment"**, only the `$` anchor separates `/cards/mtg` from `/cards/mtg/black-lotus`, and `$` cannot combine with a wildcard. **The same test run against the pre-fix file fails 38 of those checks**, so it detects the bug rather than agreeing with the new file. The 32 hubs were generated from the route configs and cross-checked against `GAME_TABLES`: **32 in each, none in one and not the other**. Live on the domain after deploy, and **card pages are blocked before and after: unchanged by this commit**. **The blog and calendar were never affected**, they are not under `/cards/` | **Closed. One maintenance hazard now written into the file itself: a 33rd game needs a line here or its hub silently closes.** Separately, and worth more than the fix: **Meta stopped. The block deployed 7 Aug 09:34 UTC, Meta did not obey immediately (there was still a 770-view hour at 14:00 UTC, it caches robots.txt), then collapsed to 3 and 4 views an hour by 16:00, and the six hours to 23:14 UTC produced ONE card view across the entire site.** So `meta-externalagent` does honour robots.txt, on a lag of several hours, which is now measured rather than taken from Meta's documentation |

| C3L-117, **shipped in full across card, set and hub pages and verified live, 2026-08-08, task `bot-diagnostics-caching-throttle-writeup`, commits `bfbb286` and `b778f2f`. What did not exist was `stale-while-revalidate` on anything except MTG card pages.** **101 of the 101 HTML page functions that register a route already set a shared-cache directive**, every one paired with a durable `Netlify-CDN-Cache-Control`. The one remaining gap, stated plainly: MTG set pages are served by `card-index.mjs`, not by a `*-set-page.mjs` file, and that function sets three different `Cache-Control` values with NO `Netlify-CDN-Cache-Control` on any of them.** So MTG set pages have **neither the durable tier nor `stale-while-revalidate`**, confirmed live at `public,s-maxage=3600`. **It was left alone deliberately: `card-index.mjs` also serves `/cards/mtg/random-commander`, which is random per request and must never be cached the same way, so its paths need separating before it can be touched safely.** **Two deliberate judgement calls recorded so they are reviewable: `finalfantasy` and `unionarena` were unified onto the common card policy** because their measured sync cadence (21.1 and 20.6 hours) is identical to everything else, so the divergence was drift; **`yugioh-hub` (300/300) and `mtg-hub` (s-maxage 3600, no max-age) were NOT flattened** and instead gained `stale-while-revalidate` additively, because their numbers could not be shown to be drift. **The browser-facing `max-age` was left untouched throughout**: it is a behaviour change for real people, not a bot-load change |
| C3L-118, **SUPERSEDED BY REALITY the same night: it is now LIVE AND ENFORCING, 2026-08-08, task `alibaba-throttle-ship`, merged as `aea1f99` and deploy-verified by `commit_ref`.** It was built and held earlier the same night on branch `c3l-107-request-throttle` at `028f34b`, then rebased onto `9d4c34f`, re-run against all 9 controls, wired into **97 page functions** and shipped. **The 30 minute monitoring window required before it could be left live passed CLEAN: 12 polls, ZERO requests throttled, no revert.** **One framing correction carried from the earlier entry: it said there was "no cost argument for shipping it", and that was true but it was the wrong axis.** The reason it shipped is **data integrity, not dollars**: while the crawl runs, GA4 and `card_views` both stop describing the real audience, which is what made every traffic question this week unanswerable. A future reader should not wonder why the decision changed; the cost finding (C3L-114) still stands and was never the argument against. **Two things it has NOT yet proven, stated so nobody reads the clean window as vindication: it has never fired in production, and the window was quiet largely because there was almost no traffic to judge** | | **An application-level request throttle now exists, and it does NOT depend on or consume Netlify's 5-rule Firewall Traffic Rule budget. That constraint was real for C3L-107's other candidate mitigations and it was never a blocker for this one.** It is application code in `shared/request-throttle.mjs`. **Nothing imports it, nothing enables it, and it is on its own branch specifically so a register push cannot carry it to main.** **It triggers on SHAPE, not on volume alone**: both conditions must hold in the same hour, more than **300 requests** from one `/24` AND more than **150 distinct paths** | **Thresholds measured from `card_views` over 6 to 7 August, the only window with request fingerprints. Busiest hour of any `/24` that was not Meta or Alibaba: 9 views. Those network-hours above 100: 0 of 18. Whole site's busiest DAY before the crawl (13 July): 93 views. Meta `57.141.18.0/24` peak hour: 982. Alibaba `43.119.100.0/24`: 647. Alibaba `47.82.201.0/24`: 577.** So 300 sits **33x above the busiest real network-hour and 3.2x above the site's best pre-crawl day**, and under every crawler hour worth catching. **Googlebot is verified by reverse DNS with forward confirmation, not by user agent**: reverse must land on `googlebot.com`, `google.com`, `search.msn.com`, `applebot.apple.com` or `duckduckgo.com`, and that hostname must resolve forward to the same address. The lookup runs **only for a block that has already blown both thresholds** and is cached 6 hours, so no ordinary page view pays for DNS. **All nine controls pass** (`scripts/test-request-throttle.mjs`): Meta's 982-request shape throttles from request 301; both Alibaba shapes throttle from 301; the response is a real **429 with `Retry-After: 3600`, `no-store` and `noindex`**; **real Googlebot at 5,000 requests is 0 throttled via LIVE reverse and forward DNS**; **a FORGED Googlebot user agent is throttled 200 times**, proving the exemption is DNS-based; 40 real sessions at 5.4 pages are untouched; and **a viral spike of 3,600 requests, 12x the request threshold, over 6 hot pages is 0 throttled** | **Held for review, and the branch is the whole point: it must not reach main by accident.** **The viral-spike control is the one that matters and is why the design is two-condition**: volume alone cannot separate a crawl from a good day, and a release-day surge is the success case for this business. **AI assistants get a raised ceiling of 1,500 rather than verification**, matched by user agent, which is free text and is not described as verification: robots.txt already welcomes these agents on purpose, throttling one costs real business, and an impostor forging the string gains only a higher limit on pages that are already free to read. **The honest limitation, matching what `account.mjs` already documents about its signup limiter: the counter is an in-memory Map in ONE serverless instance.** Netlify runs many and they cold start. **This blunts a burst from one origin and does NOT stop a crawl spread thin across many blocks or across many instances.** A counter that holds needs a shared store. **Nobody should read this as airtight**, and given C3L-114, nobody should ship it for cost reasons either |
| C3L-119, **open, needs a decision from Sammy, raised 2026-08-08, task `bot-diagnostics-caching-throttle-writeup`. An operational finding about this audit programme's own behaviour, deliberately logged as its own item rather than as a footnote to the cost row, because that is where it would be ignored** | | **This programme's own push rate is the largest single line on the Netlify bill, and it is roughly 45 times the cost of the bot traffic three tasks were written to stop. 91 production deploys landed in the first 12 days of the billing period.** At $0.10 each that is **$9.10, about 1,365 of the 3,000 included credits, spent on deploying**. Bandwidth over the same period, account-wide across all four sites, is 21.81 GB, which is **436 credits**. Verified directly: `curl` with the anon key against `sync_events` returns `[]` and HTTP 200, while the same call against `card_views`, which carries `public_read_card_views` for anon, returns rows | **Confirmed, and the fix applied here was to re-verify every block count through a service-role read rather than to change any policy.** The RLS itself is correct and should not be relaxed: `sync_events` is internal telemetry. **The general rule worth carrying: any monitor, health check or assertion that reads Supabase must be checked against a table it is KNOWN to be able to read, or its silence is meaningless.** This is the same silent-pass shape as C3L-53's three sync scripts dying behind `continue-on-error` and the C3L-111 detector whose direct-execution guard also matched its own test file. **It is written into the head of `migrations/c3l-page-views.sql`** so the next person to add a service-role-only table meets it before they build a reader for it |

Netlify resolves overlapping paths by FUNCTION NAME SORT ORDER, so moving a literal route to a differently named file can lose it to a catch-all even when the path string is byte identical.** `card-page.mjs` claims `/cards/mtg/:slug+`. While `/cards/mtg/random-commander` was declared in `card-index.mjs` it won that overlap, because `card-index` sorts before `card-page`. Moving it to `mtg-random-commander.mjs`, which sorts AFTER, lost it, and the route began returning `card-page`'s "this card's page is coming soon" 404 the moment the split deployed | **Diagnosed by reading the 404 body rather than guessing a second time.** It was `card-page`'s own template with "Random Commander" title-cased out of the slug, identical in shape to the 404 for a deliberately made-up slug, which is what identified the owner. **The first repair was WRONG and is recorded rather than quietly replaced: `priority: 1` was added to the new function**, on the reasoning that every `*-set-page.mjs` carries it to beat the same catch-all. It deployed, and the route still 404d. **The actual fix is `excludedPath` on `card-page.mjs`**, which is what the other 31 games have always used: **MTG was the ONLY game whose card page carried no `excludedPath` at all.** `/cards/mtg/sets/*` was added to that exclusion at the same time; it was never broken, but it was only unbroken by the same name-ordering accident, so leaving it implicit meant the next rename would take out every MTG set page instead of one generator | **Resolved and verified live. The caching gap C3L-117 named is now closed: `card-index.mjs` was the last page function setting no `Netlify-CDN-Cache-Control`, and its durable cache reported `fwd=bypass` on every request. Both paths now carry the durable tier and `stale-while-revalidate` with their existing durations unchanged**, and the set page shows `Netlify Durable; stored` where it previously bypassed. **The task's own premise did not survive the code and was NOT implemented: it instructed `no-store` on the random picker.** `/cards/mtg/random-commander` returns a **static shell**; `renderRandomCommander()` takes no arguments and picks nothing, and the randomness happens in the browser. The real picker is `/api/random-commander` in `card-api.mjs`, verified already correct at `no-cache` with `fwd=bypass` and 6 rapid calls returning 6 different commanders. `no-store` on the shell would have forced an origin render of unchanging HTML on every request. **A second bug was caught by RENDERING the moved function rather than parsing it**: it interpolates `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, which stayed behind in `card-index.mjs`, so the file passed `node --check` and would have thrown `ReferenceError` on first hit. **Also flagged, not fixed: the `/cards/mtg` branch inside `card-index.mjs` is dead code**, not in its config and served by `mtg-hub.mjs` instead; it now carries a comment saying so |

It produced 1. **A further internal contradiction in GA4's own data, noted rather than relied on: it reports 21,649 `user_engagement` and 15,401 `scroll` events alongside a 0 second average engagement time.** `user_engagement` normally requires ten seconds of foreground time | **Open, and the read is now explanation (b): these GA4 numbers do not correspond to page loads on this site. Confidence HIGH that the keepalive gap was not the cause, because that was directly tested and the rate did not move. Confidence MODERATE TO HIGH on (b) overall**, held back only because bandwidth cannot corroborate it either way, see **C3L-124**. **The fix is correct and stays regardless of what this traffic turns out to be**: it removed a genuine blind spot that would have distorted every future measurement. **What it does NOT do is explain the spike, and it should not be recorded as having fixed anything visible.** The next step is on the GA4 side, not the site side: check whether the property is receiving Measurement Protocol traffic against the public measurement id `G-WR68HPE92S`, which is readable in any page's source. **Scope note: the 23 static pages and two Nunjucks layouts carry an inlined copy of the tracker and were re-injected from the module's own output, so they cannot drift; `/legal` and `/pricing` carry none, verified in source and build** |

It exists because the same number was used as "inconclusive" in one place and as supporting evidence in another, which is not a defensible way to hold a figure** | | **The Netlify bandwidth number CANNOT discriminate between real traffic and fabricated analytics, and it must stop being cited as though it can.** Two hard limits, both established by probing rather than assumed: **the endpoint returns a RUNNING PERIOD TOTAL only**, with `/bandwidth/daily` and `/bandwidth/history` both 404, and a `from`/`to` query **accepted but IGNORED** (it answers `used: 0` for a 1969 period); and **there is no per-site split**, so the figure covers all four sites at once | **A time series was built from readings taken by hand, since Netlify offers none. 7 Aug 23:20 to 8 Aug 13:07: 77.0 MB/hour. 8 Aug 13:07 to 22:41: 36.5 MB/hour. 8 Aug 22:41 to 9 Aug 05:43: 58.9 MB/hour.** The decisive observation is that **the rate is roughly STEADY across all three windows and is NOT concentrated in the hours of the Singapore spike**, including the post-fix window in which C3's own instrumentation recorded about 5 page loads an hour. **It is arithmetically consistent with BOTH stories**, which is precisely why it settles nothing: 690 sessions an hour at 24.3 KB of HTML is about 17 MB/hour before assets, so 37 to 77 MB/hour could be that, and it could equally be Googlebot, the 36 sitemaps covering 363,259 URLs, the AWS HeadlessChrome bots already found in `page_views`, this programme's own diagnostics (about 535 requests on 8 August alone) and three other sites | **Resolved as a rule rather than a number: do not cite account bandwidth as evidence for or against a per-site traffic claim.** It has no time resolution finer than the readings someone remembered to take, and no site attribution at all. **The earlier "1,061 MB over 14 hours" figure is hereby retired from both uses**: it was never evidence for real traffic and it was never evidence against it. **What would actually settle it is per-site request counts, which sit behind Netlify's paid Analytics add-on**, or a log drain, which is currently unconfigured (`log_drains` returns `[]`) |

nothing in shipped code reads it with the anon key, since `card-api.mjs` writes it and `crawler-volume-check.mjs` reads it with the service key; the write path is immune on two counts because `supabasePost` defaults to the service key and sends `return=minimal` so it never asks for the row back; and the My Dashboard connector reports `tableRowCounts: {}` with no errors. **Verified live: the check's own query now returns `[]` where it returned 3 rows, the fingerprint query that returned a Perth IP returns `[]`, anon row count is 0 where it was 39,893, and all 16 tables in the check's target list now return 0 rows, so Part A would pass.** Nothing broke: 7 page types 200, both write endpoints still write, the service-role read still returns 4,747 rows over 48h. **The 17,926 already-exposed rows are deliberately left in place**: retention is a separate, lower priority decision and was explicitly held back |
| C3L-126, **found and PAUSED, verified at the deploy level, 2026-08-09, commit `3c6fd58`. Nobody could confirm whether the digest could send again. Two candidates existed and only one recurs: `generate-weekly-report.mjs`, the PAID Seller Intelligence report, is manual only at `/api/generate-weekly-report` behind a required secret, cannot self-fire, and was left untouched. **Paused by commenting out the schedule, not deleting it, so restoring is one line. The ACL now reads `postgres=X/postgres \| service_role=X/postgres` with PUBLIC gone, both cron jobs remain active as `postgres`, and all six advisor warnings for these functions have cleared. Four page types still return 200 and AUD prices still render.** **`mtg_commanders` is deliberately NOT given the same treatment.** It is anon-selectable and flagged by the same advisor, but reading it with the anon key returns 10,287 rows of **public MTG card data** (name, type line, oracle text, image, price), the same class `mtg_cards` already serves to browsers, so it exposes nothing the site does not publish itself. | C3L-129, **OPEN and NOT fixed, raised 2026-08-09. | C3L-130, **OPEN, HIGH, confirmed 2026-08-09, task `investigation-sweep-batch` item A4. Read-only, nothing fixed. The function exhausts `pg_sleep(1)` fifteen times against the primary API and fifteen against the backup, hits `v_rate IS NULL`, raises a WARNING, and returns having written nothing | **Every figure measured, not inferred.** `cron.job_run_details` for job 14: **42 `succeeded`, 2 failed, average duration 30.1 seconds**, earliest run 2026-06-27, which is the 30 second statement timeout on essentially every run. **The decisive evidence is a 42 microsecond gap**: today's run ended at `06:00:30.117407` and the newest `net._http_response` row was created at `06:00:30.117365`, so the response exists only as the function is being torn down. **`site_config.usd_aud_rate` was last written at 01:00:50, not 06:00**, proving the 06:00 run returned before its `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` line, which sets `updated_at = NOW()` unconditionally. **`net._http_response` retains only 2 rows**, so there is no history to audit earlier calls against | **Open. NOT fixed here: this file is read-only by design and a wrong fix to a price-wide calculation is worse than a slow correct one.** Three consequences, all live. **First, there are TWO writers to the FX rate and only one works.** `sync-fx-rate.mjs` is a Netlify scheduled function at `0 1 * * *` and it is the reason the rate is current at all; the Postgres cron at `0 6 * * *` is a phantom success sitting on top of it. Nobody reading pg_cron would know which one is load-bearing. **Second, the function's own "recalculate every game on a rate change" step has therefore never fired**, and it covers 21 `UPDATE <game>_cards SET price_aud = ROUND(market_price * v_rate, 2)` statements. Each game's stored AUD now reflects the rate at its own last sync instead of a common one. **Measured spread across games at one moment: 1.3831 (pokemon) to 1.4302 (yugioh) against a true rate of 1.4182, a 3.4 per cent range**, with pokemon 2.47 per cent low because its sync has been dormant since 29 July. Conversion itself is not missing: 0 of 123,781 priced cards across 7 games lack a `price_aud`. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-09, decided by Sammy: the cross-game recalculation is DROPPED and is not pending work.** Three reasons, each measured before the decision rather than argued. **First, it is already happening nightly.** Every per-game sync calls `getExchangeRate()` and writes `price_aud = market_price * audRate` on every run (`sync-yugioh-background.mjs:341`, `sync-vanguard-background.mjs:295`, and the identical line in each), so the syncs ARE the recalculation and the `return 1.45` in those files is the fallback path, not the live one. **Second, the measured benefit is nil: recalculating all 31 non-MTG tables at 1.4182 changes 1 to 17 rows by a maximum of $0.01 in 12 games**, and the rows it would touch are overwritten by that same night's sync. **The 3.4 per cent spread this row reports is not a systemic 1.45 error, it is FX drift between games synced on different days**, which is a measurement of sync timing, not of a conversion bug. **Third, for the one game where it would do something it would do the WRONG thing.** Pokemon accounts for 27,688 of the changing rows, 92.2 per cent of its catalogue, up to $716.25 each and +$40,071 in aggregate, and it is the largest game on the site. Its 1.38 implied rate is not a conversion fault, it is the rate frozen on 29 July, because `pokemon_cards.market_price` has not moved since (see C3L-133). Applying today's FX to 11 day old USD would make stale data look freshly converted without making one price more accurate. **Fix the sync and the correct AUD follows on the next run at no cost.** **Third, the client-side fallback is a hardcoded `return 1.45`, appearing 68 times across 138 files.** Against 1.4182 that is **+2.24 per cent**, applied silently to every price on any page whose 3 second `/api/fx-rate` fetch times out, with **no logging of the fallback being taken**, so its frequency is unknown and unknowable today |
| C3L-131, **OPEN, confirmed 2026-08-09, same task. The general lesson behind C3L-130, logged separately because it is not about the exchange rate and will outlive that fix** | | **A `RAISE WARNING` inside a cron-called function is INVISIBLE to pg_cron's own success and failure status.** A warning is not an error, so the function returns normally and `cron.job_run_details.status` reads `succeeded`. **Any monitoring built on that status column cannot distinguish a job that did its work from a job that gave up early and said so.** This is the same class as C3L-121, where a table with no anon policy answered 200 with an empty array, and the same class as C3L-53, where three sync scripts died for a week behind `continue-on-error` | **Scoped by inspecting all 11 cron-called functions rather than assuming the pattern is widespread or unique.** **The pg_net defect is singular: only `update_usd_aud_rate` uses `net.http_get` and `pg_sleep`**, so no other job has C3L-130's specific bug. **The warning blind spot is narrow but real: 2 of 11 functions contain `RAISE WARNING`**, `update_usd_aud_rate` and `update_mtg_signals_batched`, the latter averaging **30.5 seconds** per run, which is close enough to a timeout to be worth its own look. **The early-exit blind spot is broad: 9 of 11 carry a bare `return;`**, any of which produces a clean `succeeded` with no signal that nothing happened. Note that C3L-45 already made `update_mtg_signals_batched` distinguishable by returning the text `TRUNCATED`, but that lives in `return_message` and **not** in `status`, so a status-only check still misses it | **Open. The fix is a convention, not a patch, and it is not applied here.** A cron-called function should signal failure in a way pg_cron can see, which means `RAISE EXCEPTION` on a genuine failure rather than `RAISE WARNING`, or writing a terminal row to `sync_events` the way the Netlify background syncs already do so C3L-51's health check can read it. **Until one of those exists, `cron.job_run_details.status` should not be treated as evidence that a job worked**, and this file should stop citing it as such. **A cheap interim detector, recommended not built: assert that `site_config.usd_aud_rate.updated_at` is younger than 26 hours.** That single assertion would have caught C3L-130 on its first day instead of its forty-second, and it needs no change to any function |

CLAUDE.md is unambiguous: **"Flesh and Blood (LSS commercial restrictions, never recommend or build for this game)"** | **All seven slugs were then checked against the API's own `/v1/games` list, which knows 50, and TWO were invalid.** `lorcana` was `'disney-lorcana'`, real value `'lorcana-tcg'`. `starwars` was `'star-wars-unlimited'`, and there is **no correct value** because this API does not carry Star Wars at all, so the entry was removed rather than replaced with another guess. **The module's own comment asserted these were verified**: "These are NOT guesses: each is copied from the sync job that already pulls that game in production, which is the only place they are known to be correct." Two of seven were wrong, so **the comment claiming verification was itself false**, which is the same class as C3L-128's PROJECT.md line 618 and this register's own repeatedly-wrong row counts. **Found only because the extreme-moves work forced a dry-run that printed the game label next to each card; nothing else would have shown it**, since a mislabelled card renders perfectly well | **Resolved, and correcting the two slugs is NOT what closes it.** The structural fix is that `tcgMovers` now **validates every returned row's own `game_slug` against the slug requested** and rejects mismatches with a loud log. The response is the authority on what game a card is; the map is only a convenience. A future typo now yields NOTHING instead of another game's cards, and **Flesh and Blood is excluded by that general rule rather than by a special case naming it**. **THE GENERAL LESSON, and this is the third instance of one shape in one session: an input that is wrong but fails SILENTLY instead of erroring is more dangerous than one that breaks.** C3L-121: a Supabase table with RLS and no anon policy answers 200 with an empty array, not 403. C3L-131: a `RAISE WARNING` in a cron function leaves `cron.job_run_details.status` reading `succeeded`. This one: an unrecognised slug on a third-party API returns a global list, not a 400. **Three different systems, one failure mode, and in every case the caller had no way to tell success from silence.** The defence is the same in all three: never trust the request, check what actually came back. **One genuine gain fell out of it: Lorcana works for the first time**, with `Omnidroid - V.10` at +96.6 per cent now appearing as a real Lorcana card where the bad slug had been substituting Pokemon and FAB |

It is the largest game on the site at 31,833 cards.** The cause is one line, `sync-pokemon-background.mjs:351`: `const isNewSet = !syncedSetIds.has(set.id);`. **`pokemon_sync_progress` now holds 235 of 235 sets, so `isNewSet` is false for every set permanently and the card-write branch is unreachable.** `pokemon_cards.market_price` can never change again. What still runs each night is the snapshot-only branch at line 366, which **reads `market_price` back out of `pokemon_cards` (line 370) and re-stamps the frozen value into `pokemon_price_snapshots` with TODAY's FX rate (line 385)**, manufacturing a price history that looks live and daily and carries no new information | **Measured, not inferred, over 14 days of `pokemon_price_snapshots`.** 27 to 28 July: **5,867 distinct prices**, average USD moving 66.6333 to 66.6403. 29 July to 9 August: **6,147 or 6,148 distinct prices EVERY SINGLE DAY**, average USD flat at 69.55, across 16,968 rows written daily. **The only column that varies is `aud_rate`, 1.4384 down to 1.4182**, so Pokemon's entire AUD price movement for 11 days is currency drift on a frozen USD number. `max(pokemon_cards.updated_at)` reads **2026-07-29**, against 0 to 2 days for all 31 other games. **The audit trail built by task-67 to catch exactly this recorded `event_type='sync_success'`, `rows_affected=0`, `error_message=null` on all 7 runs in the last week**, and the run completes in about 130 seconds because it does almost nothing. **A second, independent failure sits underneath it**: `tcg_market` is NULL on 16,968 of 16,968 snapshot rows on 7 of the last 8 days, so `fetchPokemonTCGPricesForSet` is resolving no pokemontcg.io set ID for essentially every set and the secondary price source is dead too | **Open, fix authorised, deliberately NOT a flip of the condition.** `isNewSet` has no recorded rationale in either commit that introduced it, both dated 2026-05-14, but **the constraint it was answering is real and is proven by C3L-134**: Yu-Gi-Oh is the one larger game with no such gate and it cannot finish inside the 15 minute window at all. So the gate was a reasonable answer to a real limit, implemented as a **permanent one-way latch instead of a rotation**. **The fix must be staleness-based**: refresh the N most-stale sets each night within a time budget so every set is revisited on a bounded cycle, which fits the window without freezing anything. **Note the near miss on the FX side: recalculating Pokemon's `price_aud` at today's rate, which is what C3L-130's superseded step would have done, would have moved 27,688 rows by up to $716.25 and +$40,071 in aggregate and made none of them more accurate.** Staleness masquerading as a conversion bug is the trap here. **RESOLVED. `shared/sync-rotation.mjs` replaces the latch with staleness ordering plus a wall-clock budget checked BEFORE each set, so no set is begun that cannot finish.** `pokemon_sync_progress` is kept and still written; `synced_at` is now an ordering key rather than a gate, which was the specific behaviour that froze the catalogue. **First live run: 166 of 231 sets refreshed and 26,913 cards written, against ZERO on every run since 14 May.** Prices verified against an independent source rather than assumed: **pokemontcg.io matched to the cent on two newly refreshed cards, Gengar Prime 94/102 in Triumphant at 493.44 and Garchomp 120/116 in Plasma Freeze at 499**, and every refreshed row carries the upstream's own `last_price_update` stamped earlier the same day, with `aud_rate` moving off the frozen 1.38 to the live 1.4182. **Measured cadence is about 3.8s per set, far better than the deliberately pessimistic 25s reservation, so a full cycle is roughly two nights.** The second, independent fault recorded above is NOT closed by this: `tcg_market` is still NULL because `fetchPokemonTCGPricesForSet` resolves no pokemontcg.io set ID, and the secondary price source remains dead |

Three attempts, then silence for 23.4 hours. **900 seconds is the background function limit**, and the observed gaps sit just above it plus a retry delay. **The logging mechanism is known to work**, because Pokemon emits `sync_success` through the identical helper, so Yu-Gi-Oh's absence is real rather than a missing instrumentation. Comparison across all 32 games in the same window: **every other game has starts equal to successes plus errors**, with real row counts; only Yu-Gi-Oh has starts with no terminal event at all. Two games complete but with intermittent failures, weiss-schwarz at 4 successes to 3 errors and star-wars-unlimited at 5 to 2 | **Open. The data is NOT visibly wrong, which is why this survived: `yugioh_cards` shows 0 days stale**, because each killed attempt still writes whatever it got through before the wall. **So Yu-Gi-Oh is being partially updated every night, forever, and it is far worse than "partially" implies: because each of the three attempts restarts from the SAME position, the head sets are refreshed repeatedly and the tail starves.** Measured 9 August: **248 of 615 sets written that day, while 365 sets holding about 21,500 cards, 46 per cent of the game, were last written between 7 June and 11 July.** **CORRECTION, same day: this row originally said "nothing anywhere says so", and that was FALSE.** `scripts/sync-health-check.mjs` **has been detecting both this and C3L-133 precisely, and failing, on every run it has ever done.** Its 8 August output names them exactly: `yugioh::sync started 13.9h ago, no success or error since` and `pokemon 10d 2026-07-29 STALE METADATA`. **The instrumentation is not missing. It was built for C3L-51, it works, and it found both of these before anyone asked it to.** What is missing is anyone reading what it says, which is a different problem with a different fix and is logged as C3L-135. What IS true as written is narrower: the failure is invisible **from the data**, since `max(yugioh_cards.updated_at)` reads today and no column records which sets are months old. **This is the same fix as C3L-133 and they should be built together**: a staleness-ordered rotation with a time budget turns an unbounded crawl that dies into a bounded one that finishes and records that it finished. **It is also the fourth instance in one session of the shape START HERE now leads with**, and the purest one **at the function level**: the process that would have reported the failure is the same process that was killed, so success and silence are literally indistinguishable from inside. **That is exactly why an EXTERNAL check is the right shape for this class, and why it is worth being precise that the external check here already existed and already worked.** **RESOLVED by the same rotation.** Ordering by staleness is what makes the three nightly attempts ADD UP instead of repeat, since a stopped run resumes where it stopped rather than from the top. **First live run: 254 of 615 sets, 20,381 cards, and the first terminal `sync_events` row Yu-Gi-Oh has emitted in at least 7 days.** It now writes the line the fix exists to produce, `partial rotation: 254/615 sets refreshed, 701s budget spent, oldest unrefreshed 2026-05-15`, which is precisely what makes a budget-stopped run distinguishable from a killed one. **The starved tail went from 365 sets holding about 21,500 cards, last written between 7 June and 11 July, down to 214 sets and 11,291 cards in ONE run.** Spot-checked independently against YGOPRODeck by set code and rarity: **RP02-EN096 Secret Rare, WP11-EN012 Super Rare and CP08-EN005 Super Rare**, the last listed here as "Champion Pack 8" against YGOPRODeck's "Champion Pack: Game Eight", a naming variant rather than a mismatch. **One honest limitation on the first run only: all 611 progress rows carried the SAME 15 May stamp, so the ordering key was uninformative and the tiebreak fell back to set id.** It self-corrects within about three nights as real stamps accumulate, which is why it was not worth a seeding migration. **A known imprecision, harmless because it cannot affect a scheduled run: in manual sample mode the summary's denominator is the requested cap, so a capped run reports itself complete** |

| **Two independent, measured instances, which is what makes this a pattern rather than an oversight.** **First: `sync-health-check.yml`, 5 runs, 5 failures, 0 actioned.** Its 8 August output reads `FAIL: 1 sync stream(s) started without ever finishing: yugioh::sync (13.9h)` and `FAIL: 1 game(s) with card metadata stale by 4+ days: pokemon (10d, last 2026-07-29)`. **Second, and older: `daily-mtg-sync.yml` failed 7 consecutive nights, 29 July to 4 August**, each run exiting 1 with a red tick and the explicit line `FATAL: Error: Could not find default_cards bulk URL`. **That is 6 days of MTG price history lost, on the site's largest catalogue at 98,913 cards, with a correct and unambiguous alert firing nightly throughout.** Both were eventually fixed by a person happening to look, which C3L-51's own header already names as "not a detection strategy" | **Open, and NOT closable by code.** The detection layer is now good and adding more of it would make this worse, not better: a fifth alert nobody reads has negative value, and C3L-51's own note is that a threshold which cries wolf gets ignored. **What is missing is an owner and a response expectation**, meaning a named person who reads GitHub's failure email, a stated window in which a red workflow is triaged, and somewhere durable to record that a given failure was seen and judged. **The evidence that this is the binding constraint rather than a nice-to-have: every technical control in this programme worked as designed in both instances above, and the outcome was still 6 days of lost MTG data and 4 days of | C3L-136, **OPEN, MEDIUM, confirmed 2026-08-09 by direct HTTP test against production, not inferred. A NON-scheduled function, `card-api`, returns **404** on the same host, which is a routing answer rather than a refusal, so the 403 is specific to the presence of the `schedule` key and not to the host, the path or the deploy context. **The decisive proof is the rotation sample run in task 36: the identical function on the identical host became invocable the moment `schedule` was temporarily swapped for `path` on a branch, and went back to 403 when the schedule was restored.** | **Open, and NOT fixed here. It is a decision about 45 files, which is a task in its own right rather than a side effect of a sync repair.** Three things are true at once and need separating before anyone acts. **The dead code is harmless in itself**, since a request that never arrives cannot misbehave. **The comments are actively harmful**, because they describe a capability that does not exist, and the next person who needs a manual trigger will lose time proving otherwise. **The missing capability is real**: there is no way to run any sync on demand today, which is precisely what made verifying C3L-133 and C3L-134 awkward and forced a temporary branch-only workaround. **The likely fix is ONE authenticated trigger endpoint that is not itself scheduled and dispatches to the sync functions**, rather than 45 separate corrections, but that is a design decision for Sammy and is not made here. **Same shape as C3L-132 and C3L-128, a comment asserting something was verified when it was not.** This one survived longer than either because nothing fails when unreachable code stays unreached |

Nothing has ever seen it. **Two INDEPENDENT blockers, either of which alone is sufficient**, which is why it survived: the page query filters `rarity=neq.None` while every sealed product carries exactly `rarity = 'None'`, and the same query never selects `low_price`, so `p.low_price` is permanently `undefined` | **Measured, and it is uniform, not a single broken page.** `rarity=neq.None` appears in **28 of 28** set pages; the sealed block appears in **28 of 28**; and **0 of 28** select `low_price`. For Lorcana, **114 of 114** rows matching the sealed name filter carry `rarity = 'None'`, so the filter excludes precisely the rows the block exists to display. Confirmed against the live site and not merely read from source: fetching `/cards/lorcana/sets/the-first-chapter` returns no `Low:` string and no `~AU$` figure anywhere, and the only "Sealed" text on the page belongs to the nav dropdown. **138,802 rows across the seven games sampled do carry a `low_price`**, so the data exists and is simply never requested | **Open. Deliberately NOT scoped here, and NOT to be fixed as part of the FX work.** Whether this block should work at all is a commercial question, not a bug report: sealed product is a real revenue surface given `/shop` and the eBay affiliate placements, so the options genuinely include building it properly and deleting it, and neither is Claude Code's call. **What is already decided is that it must not be left silently half-present.** Note the shape, which is C3L-136 one step further on: not a comment claiming a capability that does not exist, but an entire feature that does not run, with nothing anywhere failing to say so. **It was found only because the FX work touched a line inside it and live verification asked whether that line renders.** Reading the code would never have revealed it, because the code is correct; the query feeding it is what makes it unreachable. **RESOLUTION, Task 38, 2026-08-10.** The finding's own framing was checked before anything was changed, and **one half of it does not survive: `rarity = 'None'` is NOT a sealed-product marker.** It is an absent-rarity marker and it is **overloaded three ways**: genuine sealed product, non-card accessories (Dragon Ball energy markers, Lorcana puzzle inserts, Digimon index cards), and **ordinary singles** (essentially all 529 Shadowverse `None` rows are singles, as are Lorcana's `Sand Wave` and `Palace Guard - Crown Hunter`). **Across the 28 games, 6,353 rows carry `rarity` `None` or NULL and only 2,949 of them, 46.4 per cent, match the block's own sealed name filter.** So filtering the page ON `rarity = 'None'` would have shipped singles and accessories into a block headed "Buy Sealed Product". **A second exclusion the finding did not name: `rarity=neq.None` in PostgREST also drops `rarity IS NULL`, and six games (Weiss Schwarz, Vanguard, Force of Will, Buddyfight, WoW, Dragon Ball Z) hold ZERO `'None'` rows and 1,750 NULL ones**, so on those six the block was unreachable by a route the finding's `rarity = 'None'` explanation does not cover at all. **What shipped instead: a SECOND, separate query per set page** for `or=(rarity.eq.None,rarity.is.null)` ordered `market_price.desc.nullslast`, selecting `low_price`, feeding the sealed block only. **The singles query is untouched**, so the singles list, top 5, movers and rarity filter all keep their existing contents, and the existing name-keyword filter (which never depended on rarity) stays the thing that decides what counts as sealed | 
| C3L-138, **CLOSED SAME DAY, and recorded because I CAUSED IT, 2026-08-09. Introduced in `e16d00e` and reverted in `eb1db70` before any user could have been shown a wrong figure for long. Logged in full rather than quietly reverted, because the reasoning error is the useful part** | | **A "fix" to `card-compare.mjs` made foil prices render about 2.2 per cent LOW on a page whose entire purpose is comparing prices.** The original code read `fetchCard(game, slug, 1.45)` while `getLiveRate()` sat in the same `Promise.all`, which looks exactly like a page fetching the live rate and then ignoring it. **It was not.** The 1.45 was a deliberate INTERMEDIATE denomination, divided back out and replaced with the live rate a few lines later: `priceAudFoil: c.priceAudFoil ? (c.priceAudFoil / 1.45 * usdToAud) : null`. Passing the live rate into `fetchCard` broke that invariant and made the same line apply the rate TWICE, yielding `usd * rate squared / 1.45` | **The arithmetic, stated plainly. Correct AUD is `usd * 1.4182`.** After the change it became `usd * 1.4182 * 1.4182 / 1.45`, which is `usd * 1.3871`, so every foil price on `/compare` was understated by **2.15 per cent**. **A second call site depends on the same invariant**: `renderCompareTable` at line 693 derives its `data-usd` attribute as `c.priceAudFoil / 1.45`, and that function does not receive `usdToAud` at all, so a correct fix requires threading the rate through another function rather than editing one line. **Found by LIVE verification after deploy, not by review**: `/compare` was the only page still containing a `1.45` once every other page had stopped, and chasing that one anomaly is what forced reading the conversion chain properly | **Closed by reverting to the working state, not by attempting a better fix at the end of a long batch.** `card-compare.mjs` is therefore **the one file where shape 2 is not closed**, and that is recorded rather than rounded up to done. **THE LESSON, and it is not "be careful": a hardcoded constant that looks wrong in isolation can be load-bearing, and the way to tell is to follow the value to where it is CONSUMED rather than judging it where it is written.** Every other 1.45 in this task was a terminal value; this one was an intermediate, and it read identically. **A second, sharper lesson: the change passed `node --check`, passed module import, passed a render test, and was still wrong**, because all three verify that code RUNS, not that its arithmetic means what it did before. Only comparing the live rendered figure against the previous one would have caught it, which is exactly the check that did |
| C3L-139, **OPEN, and it is about THIS FILE, confirmed 2026-08-09.
